# Design decisions

Why cornercase works the way it does, area by area. The rules every change follows are in [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md).

## Code layout (`src/`, `tests/layers.rs`)

- **By domain**: code is grouped by what it is about. Each domain has one folder (or a file and its folder), named like its section in this file. A domain holds its model (its data and pure rules) and the background jobs that feed it (git, HTTP, reading files). Its drawing lives in `ui/` and its wiring into `App` in `app/`. The tree moves there one step at a time, and new code goes where these rules say. Left out: folders by layer (domain, infrastructure, app, ui), a crate workspace, and a rewrite of `App` as traits, plugins or messages. Each would change every file at once, when the only problem is where code goes. Modules are not renamed for taste: `control` stays `control`, `state` stays `state`, and a module keeps its form (`foo.rs` beside `foo/`, or `foo/mod.rs`).
- **`lib.rs` in five groups** (#197): its `pub mod` lines form five blocks, separated by blank lines and unnamed in the code, which has no comments. (1) The shared leaves: `error`, `gh`, `http`, `list`, `log`, `panics`, `paths`, `persist`, `process`, `text`, `time` and `version`. (2) The domains, each a model and its own I/O jobs: `agents` (the known agents, and `activity`, `awake`, `context`, `launch`, `stats` and `usage`), `changes`, `config`, `discord` (`gateway`, `markdown`), `files`, `flows` (`brief`, `builtin`, `dialog`, `folder`, `progress`, `registry`, `run`, `share`, `start`, `store`), `git` (`remote`, `upstream`, `worktree`), `graphics`, `highlight` (`markdown`, `syntax`), `host` (the outer terminal: `clipboard`, `notify`, `pointer`, `theme`), `issues` (`secrets` among the trackers, and `html`, shared by Plane and Asana), `messenger` (`link`, `markup`), `plugins` (`manifest`, `registry`, `run`, `supervise`), `project` (`group`, `hierarchy`, `picker`, `search`, `split`, `view`, `vscode`), `pull_request` (`merged`), `settings`, `shortcuts`, `star`, `state`, `telegram` (`html`, `link`), `term` (`emulator`, `keys`, `memory`, `mouse`), `todo`, `queue` and `update` (`restart`). (3) The view, `ui`. (4) The application, `app`. (5) The processes: `cli`, `client`, `control`, `protocol`, `remote`, `server` and `web` (`tailscale`). `web` sits with the processes rather than the domains: it serves the window to a browser from inside the server as one of its clients (its bridge sends `Hello` and decodes input with `client::input::Parser`), and only browser access uses `tailscale`. A child module keeps its folder, which holds its tests and, for `agents/context/` and `agents/usage/`, its children; every item stayed as reachable as before, through a longer path, until the next step narrowed `pub` (below). Only one file was renamed in that move, `host_theme.rs` to `host/theme.rs`; its types kept their names (`HostTheme`, `ThemeProbe`). The move was made by a script, kept in the description of its pull request, so that a branch open at the time could run it over its own changes.
- **`pub` is what the binary and the e2e tests use** (#197): every module, item and field is `pub(crate)` or narrower, except what `src/main.rs` (`cli::{run, Cli}`, `error::Error`) and `tests/e2e/` reach, with the modules on its path and the types in its signatures and fields: `agents::claude`'s Claude variables, `agents::stats` and `agents::Known` (the e2e test writes a `stats.json`), `control`'s status report and pane rows, `paths::SOCKET_ENV`, `protocol::{NESTED_ENV, own_uid}`, `project::split::{Dir, Node}` and `project::group::GROUP_STYLES`, `host::clipboard::osc52`, `update::{LATEST_ENV, target}`, `version::CURRENT`, `config::Sidebar`, `state::Widths`, and the `ui` geometry the e2e tests click by (`layout`, `Areas`, the buttons, rows and `todo::rows`). The compiler never reports a `pub` item of a library as unused, since another crate could use it, and with everything `pub` (about 1,040 items, 680 methods and 1,100 fields) dead code stayed. `unreachable_pub = "warn"` in `Cargo.toml` keeps it so: a new item is `pub(crate)` unless the binary or the e2e tests need it, and `dead_code` then reports what nothing uses. Narrowing found little dead code, deleted in the same change: `files::Panel::indexed_files`, `Lines::is_empty` in `files`, `shortcuts::Prefix::event`, `todo::Removed::is_empty`, `web::Web::browsers`, `ui`'s `list_item_at`, and re-exports nothing read (`app::Start`, `ui::{AGENTS_LABEL, TODO_LABEL, files_button, scrollback_label}`). What only the tests use (some `ui` geometry, `git::main_worktree`, `graphics::decode::HEAD`, `web::Web::listening`, a few methods) stays where it is under `#[cfg(test)]`, so tests keep taking positions from the geometry next to the drawing code. Variants and fields of serialized types would keep an `#[expect(dead_code, reason = "…")]` rather than go, since they are read by other programs or older versions; none was reported. Clippy's `avoid-breaking-exported-api` (on by default) skipped some lints on exported items; once crate-private they apply: `term::mouse::encode` takes its `MouseEvent` by value (`trivially_copy_pass_by_ref`), and the keys request's `keys` field keeps its JSON name under an `#[expect(clippy::struct_field_names)]`.
- **One folder per agent** (#198, PR 1; `grok/` in #241 and `cursor/` in #290 followed the same shape): `agents/{claude,codex,gemini,opencode}/` holds each agent's existing activity rules, record readers, last-message readers, plan probes where supported, and test fake. Whole files move with their tests; mixed files are cut by ownership. `agents.rs` keeps the catalogue, modes, arguments, detection, trust and resume rules; `activity.rs` keeps shared activity, status, notifications and rollups; `context.rs` keeps its existing pane state and update methods. Discovery, JSONL, counting and backwards-message helpers stay in `context/`, and the probe runner and dialog state stay in `usage/`. Item names and bodies stay unchanged; callers use the new paths without compatibility re-exports. Claude's module exposes the two environment constants the e2e tests need; the other agent modules are crate-visible. `activity` is also crate-visible now that those constants moved out and no external caller needs it. Helpers shared only within agents use `pub(in crate::agents)`; plan-probe fixtures used by app tests and fake modules are crate-visible under `cfg(test)`. Tests follow their subject; the record-selection and subagent-counting tests remain beside `context::Pane` to check shared dispatch and its counting ledger. The session helper used by activity tests stays with Claude's tests, and the writer used by both message readers stays with the shared backwards tests. Fixtures and snapshot files keep their paths and bytes. Identity dispatch and reader ownership are described under Agents and Context use.
- **Shared leaves** (`error`, `gh`, `log`, `panics`, `process`, `version`, `paths`, `time`, `persist`, `text`, `list`, `http`) import only the standard library, external crates and other leaves. `tests/layers.rs` checks their production files and allows their tests to use domains. Helpers move with their callers, so domains import the leaves directly, and so do the e2e tests (`version::CURRENT`, `paths::SOCKET_ENV`). `persist::Saver` has no default type because a leaf cannot name the session model; its server field names `State` explicitly. `CORNERCASE_SOCKET` is read in one place (`paths::custom_socket`, used by `socket`, the files that sit next to the socket and `protocol::check_socket_dir`), `config` and `session` share one `$XDG_*_HOME`-or-`$HOME` fallback, and each file keeps its own name rule. `time.rs` holds one calendar (`days_from_civil`, `civil_from_days`); `Stamp::parse` still answers `None` before 1970. OSC 52 and Jira's Basic header use the `base64` crate, as the kitty, iTerm2 and stats code already did, so `host::clipboard` no longer has an encoder of its own. The savers keep their policies.
- **Models never use `app` or `ui`**: only `app` and `server` use `crate::app`. Only `ui`, `app`, `server` and `client` (the reconnecting notice) use `crate::ui`. A model that needs something from `ui` (a label helper, a preset, a width) moves it into the model or into a shared module. A view built from a model's state is built in `app`. ratatui's own types (`Style`, `Color`, `Rect`, `Line`) are not `ui`, so the modules that use them keep them: the emulator, highlighting, Markdown, the diff, and split and pane geometry. Before this rule, `settings` and `usage` imported `ui` while `ui` imported them, and `term` and `web` imported `app`. So a dialog's model and its drawing could only change together.
- **Dialog models own state and rules**; the TODO panel, settings input and the issues browser show the pattern. Models take sizes and semantic hits, and `app/<name>_dialog.rs` builds and hit-tests the `ui` view. Texts decided by the model stay plain strings there; a view built in one piece and the tests that read it move together. The remaining dialog view builders migrate one model at a time.
- **Session and config types belong to models** (#193): `project/group.rs` owns `Group`, `GroupEntry` and group styles; `config.rs` owns `Sidebar` and `Tabs`; `state.rs` owns the serialized `Widths` fields; `agents.rs` owns `AgentState`. Their derives and serde attributes stay unchanged, and config keeps storing strings. `ui/widths.rs` keeps the width default and geometry impls, which need its constants and `ui::Border`; Rust permits those impls elsewhere in the same crate. Models, the app and the e2e tests import them from their owners; the re-exports in `ui.rs` and `state.rs` that kept the old paths went in #197.
- **`impl App` only in `app.rs` and under `src/app/`**: Rust lets only the module that defines a struct, and that module's child modules, see its private fields. An `impl App` in, say, `changes/` would need `App`'s fields to be `pub(crate)`. `app.rs` keeps the struct, `App::new`, the methods the server calls, the refresh pipeline, and dispatchers whose arms are one call each. Everything else goes in one child of `app` per concern. The split keeps item bodies and public paths unchanged. Private items shared by children or tests use `pub(super)` (nested children use `pub(in crate::app)`); fields of moved structs use `pub(super)` because siblings cannot see private fields. The fields of `App` stay private. Children import moved items from their defining module, so the switchboard does not become an import registry for the whole app.
- **A feature is three files with one name**: its model (`todo/`), its wiring (`app/todo_panel.rs`, or `app/<name>_dialog.rs` for a dialog) and its drawing (`ui/todo.rs`). The suffix also keeps a child of `app` from taking the name of a module that `app.rs` imports by name (`todo`, `changes`, `files`, `search`, `settings`), which Rust refuses (E0255). In `ui/`, a widget keeps its view types, geometry, hit testing and drawing in one file. Drawing and hit testing share the geometry (see Layout), and a widget spread over three places in `ui.rs` was hard to change in one go.
- **State goes in its concern's file; a background job starts through `App::spawn_job`** (`app/jobs.rs`): the work, and the closure that turns its result into an `AppEvent`, are all a feature writes. Three kinds of thread stay by hand: the file read, which answers twice, the Tailscale worker, which runs its orders one after another on purpose, and the plugin supervisors, which run for as long as the server or the window (see Plugins).
- **App state belongs to its concern** (#194, PR 3): `App` holds six groups instead of listing each concern's fields centrally. Each group owns its original defaults and environment reads; list scrolls and upstream pacing use `Default`. The rest of the core state, the panels and test knobs stay flat.

  | Field | Type and owner | State |
  |---|---|---|
  | `issues` | `IssueConnections`, `app/issues_dialog.rs` | Issue and people caches, accounts, connection epochs, API endpoints, token variables and secrets path, remembered browser tab, closed switch and people filters. |
  | `update` | `UpdateState`, `app/update_dialog.rs` | `updates`, changelog `scroll`, `restart_list` and when it was `listed`. |
  | `agents` | `AgentRuntime`, `app/agent_watch.rs` | Pending launches, cached trust pattern, last `watched` time, awake lock, `claude_dir` and stats. |
  | `outbox` | `Outbox`, `app.rs` | Host writes, notifications, detach and restart requests taken by the server. |
  | `scroll` | `Scrolls`, `app/layout.rs` | Projects, workspaces, agents and tab-bar offsets, and the `followed` focus. |
  | `upstream` | `Upstream`, `app/worktrees.rs` | Fetch times, projects being counted and the last count time. |

  The child types and their fields are `pub(super)`; `App`'s fields and its `Outbox` fields stay private. Nothing becomes visible outside `app`. The two `Outbox` types have different owners: the app queues effects, while `server::Outbox` writes one client's messages, so their module context keeps them distinct. Functions keep borrowing individual fields, such as `agents.stats` and `agents.claude_dir` during a mutable walk of `projects`; adding helper methods to these groups would borrow a whole group. Copying now uses `App::copy` after a pane lookup returns a plain reference (see Hierarchy). Server-facing signatures stay the same (`take_host_writes`, `take_notifications`, `take_detach`, `take_restart`, `stats`, `set_stats`, `set_issue_cache`). Test edits only follow the new field paths; their behaviour, names and snapshots stay the same.

- **One drag at a time** (#194, PR 4): `App::drag: Option<Drag>` replaces the independent drag fields, including the files panel's line-selection anchor. `Drag` lives in `app/interaction.rs` beside its payloads and is `Clone`, not `Copy`; its variants are `Border`, `Divider`, `Row`, `Pane`, `Selection`, `Link` and `Lines`. Starting one replaces the previous drag and resetting interaction clears it once. Dispatch, key cancellation, pointer shapes, link hover and the view exhaustively decide which variants they handle; the row accessor does the same for tick and auto-scroll readers. `Selection` carries an optional path press so selecting text and opening an unmoved path share one state. `Link` passes through drag dispatch to pane mouse handling, retaining the underline while a mouse-reading program's press waits. `Esc` and the prefix key cancel only border, divider, row and pane drags; text and file-line selections continue. Border and divider double-click history stays separate because it outlives the drag. The enum and payloads are visible only inside `app`; server methods, serialized state and `ui::Drag` keep their signatures and paths.

- **One side panel at a time** (#194, PR 5): `App::side: Option<Side>` replaces the three panels' independent `open` flags. `Side` lives in `app/layout.rs` and is visible only inside `app`. `show_side` closes the previous side through its existing close method before selecting the next; selecting the same side does nothing, so opening a path in an already open Files panel keeps its search state. Switching commits a TODO edit and unfocuses a changes filter or files search. `close_panel` drops the TODO field first so `Esc` still cancels it. The panel models keep their contents and places without owning visibility. `refresh_files`, before `refresh_changes`, sets `changes.watched` when Changes is selected or Files is visible, preserving the one-second refresh pace. Restore prefers Files, then TODO, then Changes when saved flags conflict; save keeps the existing three booleans, omission rules and session version. Trace still logs `panel shown=changes|todo|files|none`. `Side::Flow` (#263) came later as a fourth side, with a fourth flag of its own (see Layout).

- **Dialogs dispatch once per overlay** (#194, PR 6): a dialog is an `Overlay` variant with its view and mouse handling in its own `app/` file. `overlay_view`, `overlay_mouse` and `submit_form` keep one-call arms or plain values, so adding a dialog adds its variant and a line to each dispatcher. The name forms and closing confirmations share `app/dialogs.rs`; confirmations use one builder with their own title, message and labels. Menus live in `app/menus.rs`, usage clicks and sharing in `app/usage_dialog.rs`, and remote clicks in `app/remote_access.rs`. Mouse dispatch matches a borrowed overlay, so it neither clones state nor removes the dialog before hit testing. It checks a left press outside first, then delegates; a menu closes on any press and acts only on a left one, while forms act only on left presses. Submission takes the overlay and each working arm returns its successor, including workspace validation and creation in `app/worktrees.rs`; a busy or otherwise unhandled overlay is returned unchanged. Helpers shared between children stay `pub(super)`, and submission helpers used only by `dialogs.rs` stay private. Server methods, keyboard dispatch, snapshots and serialized state keep their existing contracts.

- **Job answers belong to their domain** (#194): `AppEvent` (`app/event.rs`) wraps `WorktreeEvent`, `IssueEvent`, `ChangesEvent`, `FilesEvent` and `UpdateEvent`, defined and answered in `app/worktrees.rs`, `app/issues_dialog.rs`, `app/changes_panel.rs`, `app/files_panel.rs` and `app/update_dialog.rs`. Adding an answer changes only its domain's enum, `name()` and handler. Each enum is `pub` and derives `Debug`, since it is a payload of the public `AppEvent`; `app.rs` imports their names for `app/event.rs` and the app tests. `AppEvent::name()` delegates to the nested enum and keeps the existing words on `server.log`'s `job answered` lines. Every `handle_event` arm is one call; input filtering and PTY output stay in methods in `app.rs`, keeping the server's `Input`, `Output` and `Exited` variants and the mappings from `term::Event` and `web::Event`. Issue epochs are checked in `issue_answered`: stale lists, reads and people are dropped, while a stale token check still answers an error so its form stops waiting.
- **Models send their own events** (#193): `term` and `web` do not name `AppEvent`. `term::Event` (`Output`, `Exited`) and `web::Event` are theirs, and `Term::spawn` and `Web::new` take a `Sender<E>` for any `E: From<…>`; `app/event.rs` maps them onto `AppEvent` with `From`, so `Server` and `App` still match `AppEvent::Output`. `web`'s `Context` holds the sender as an `Arc<dyn Fn(Event)>`, so it and the bridge stay non-generic. The server's identity in every shell (`CORNERCASE`, `CORNERCASE_PANE`, `CORNERCASE_SERVER`) is built by `App::pane_env` and passed in `SpawnOptions::env`, ahead of the caller's own entries; `term` only sets `TERM` and strips Claude Code's session variables, which apply to any shell.
- **Tests in a file of their own**: the tests of `foo.rs` (or `foo/mod.rs`) go in `foo/tests.rs`, declared with `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;`. Large ones keep their shared helpers there and put each group in `foo/tests/<group>.rs`. They stay child modules because they use private fields and methods, which the integration tests in `tests/` cannot see. A snapshot lives in `snapshots/` next to the file whose test calls `assert_snapshot!`, which is where insta looks for it. Its name comes from the module path, which a move keeps. A file that still holds an inline `mod tests { … }` keeps it until its tests move out, in a commit that changes nothing else.
- **At most 1,000 lines per file**, in `src/` and `tests/`, tests included. `app.rs` grew to about 17,000 lines and was touched by half of all commits, so branches working on unrelated features kept meeting there.
- **Moving is not changing**: a commit that moves code changes no behaviour. The same tests pass, and snapshots keep their names and what they render. Duplicates that differ on purpose stay apart. Old paths keep resolving through `pub use` until their callers move. So a move can be reviewed as a move.
- **`tests/layers.rs`** checks the imports and the line limit on every `cargo test`. It reads the sources under `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` and leaves out comments, strings and test code (`#[cfg(test)]` items, `tests` modules, `test_util`), since tests may take positions from `ui`'s geometry. It resolves each `crate::`, `super::` and `self::` path to the module it reaches, inline modules included. The files that broke a rule when the check was added are its exceptions. The lists only shrink: an entry that no longer applies fails the test until it is removed, and nothing is ever added (a file that moves renames its entry). An import exception covers its file and the folder of the same name next to it, so splitting `foo.rs` into children under `foo/` needs no new entry, while an entry for `foo/mod.rs` covers only that file and the rest of `foo/` stays checked. A size exception covers its own file only: every file a split creates must already be under the limit. A parser crate (`syn`) was left out: as a dev-dependency it would change `Cargo.toml`, which makes a pull request a release, and a small scanner does the job.
- **jscpd sees every file**: `.jscpd.json` sets `maxLines` and `maxSize` far above any file. By default jscpd skips files over 1,000 lines or 100 KB, and it counts lines with `split("\n")`, so a 1,000-line file ending in a newline is already over. That left out the 14 largest files, `app.rs` and `ui.rs` among them, which hold more than half of the lines. So jscpd reported 0.43 % of duplication where there was 0.87 % (October 2026).

- **`ui/` is one file per part of the screen** (#191): `ui.rs` keeps `View`, `draw()` and the `mod` and `use` lines, and each child (`sidebar`, `workspaces`, `tree`, `agents_section`, `drop`, `chrome`, `pane`, `toast`, `dialog` with `dialog/`) holds its part's view types, geometry, hit testing and drawing, so a widget is no longer spread over three stretches of one file. The children are private modules and `ui.rs` re-exports every public name from them, so `ui::X` stays the one public path. Helpers several parts share live in `widgets.rs`, `theme.rs` and `rows.rs`; the older children (`changes`, `files`, `todo`, `tab_bar`, `remote_access`) keep reaching them as `super::X` through a private `use` in `ui.rs`. Items other files use are `pub(super)` (`pub(in crate::ui)` in `dialog/` when `ui.rs` or another part uses them), the rest stay private; the few fields and methods of `Rows`, `Band`, `Tags`, `Spot` and `Search` that other parts reach are widened the same way. The tests stay grouped by behaviour in `ui/tests/`, not by file, so a move never renames a snapshot.

## Layout (`config.rs`, `state.rs`, `ui.rs`, `ui/`, `app/layout.rs`, `app/sidebar.rs`, `app/tab_bar.rs`, `app/interaction.rs`)

- `sidebar` in `config.json` lays out the lists (wide mode only; below 90 columns the compact menu below applies to every value): `projects_on_top` (the default since 0.9.0, `config::Sidebar`'s `#[default]`, so unknown values fall back to it), `workspaces_on_top`, `side_by_side` and `tree`. One column was made the default because it gives the panes the most room; a `config.json` that names `side_by_side` keeps it (settings save the whole file, so anyone who changed any setting before 0.9.0 keeps three columns). App tests pin `side_by_side` in `new_app`, since their geometry helpers assume it; `tests/e2e/main.rs` lays out its geometry with `Sidebar::default()`, so e2e covers the shipped default.
- Side by side: projects (32), workspaces (26), pane (after one blank column). Columns are always present so PTY sizes do not jump. Borders drag to resize (double click resets), within `MIN_COLUMN_WIDTH` and `MIN_PANE_WIDTH`; `Widths::fit` squeezes them on small terminals. Widths are saved in the session. PTYs follow the drag live.
- **Stacked sidebar** (`projects_on_top`, `workspaces_on_top`): one column of `Widths::projects` holds both lists, split by a line that drags (`Border::Stack`; `Widths::stack` = rows of the top section, `None` = half, each section at least `MIN_STACK_SECTION`), with one footer anchored at the bottom (issues and changes, files and todo, settings, usage, quit). `stacked_layout` fills the same `Areas`, so drawing and hit testing are shared; `Areas::sidebar` and `Areas::workspaces` are the two sections, so the wheel scrolls the list under it. Column borders are drawn from their `Areas` rects, not from `Block`s, so a stacked column gets one full-height border.
- **Tree** (`tree`): one column on the same frame and footer as the stacked layouts (`ui::layout::one_column`, `ui::layout::tree_layout`, `Areas::tree`) with one list, `ui::TreeRow` rows from a plain `ui::TreeShape` that `App::tree_shape` builds and `View::tree` carries: group headers, projects, for an unfolded project its workspaces and a `+ New` row (the workspaces' menu, see Hierarchy), for an unfolded workspace its tabs and `+ Tab`; `+ New` is also the sticky button, the projects' one. Content starts at `2 + 2·depth`. The arrow (`▾`/`▸`, two cells) folds a project or workspace on release (`RowDrag::fold`, so rows still drag); the rest of the row activates it and unfolds it; a group header folds anywhere, as elsewhere. One row holds the `▌` and the surface: the active tab, else its nearest shown ancestor (`ui::tree_active_row`); the active project and workspace are bold white. Folded projects show counts and `agents::activity::attention`; a folded workspace shows `agents::activity::highest` (see Agent status); open ones leave it to their tabs. `+ Tab` under another project switches to it; a project's `+ New` row acts for that project without switching (its menu, the form and the start dialog carry its id, and switching behind a form the user may cancel would change the pane). Drops follow the other lists' rules on one row list (`ui::tree_drop`): the row under the pointer counts as its owner at the dragged row's level, a project lands after a whole project block, workspaces and tabs stay in their parent; the landing line takes the dragged level's indent. The App learns the tree is on from `Areas::tree`, never from the config (below 90 columns the layout is compact whatever the setting), and keeps the last drawn layout in `App::drawn` (its `Areas::tree`), so `follow` reveals the active row again when the layout changes. `View::tree` labels only what is unfolded: a tab's label looks up processes and a workspace's reads `.git/HEAD`.
- **Agents section** (`agents_section` in `config.json`, Settings → UI, off by default): one list of every pane running Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI or opencode, in every project, at the bottom of the sidebar, so an agent that needs you is one click away whatever project is shown; the status icons on project rows said that something needed you, not which agent, and finding it meant three clicks. Off by default so the sidebar of someone who runs no agents stays as it was; off, every layout is drawn exactly as before (the existing snapshots did not change). `ui::full_layout` takes the switch (`layout_with` is the same without it, for the callers that never show it). It sits below the lists and above the footer, after a line that drags (`Border::Agents`; `Widths::agents` = its rows, title included, saved in the session; `None` = a third of the room; at least `MIN_STACK_SECTION`, and the lists keep theirs: `ui::layout::split_agents` with a `keep` of one section for side by side and the tree, two and their line when stacked). In side by side it takes the bottom of the projects column; in the one-column layouts `ui::layout::one_column` cuts it off the room above the footer, so stacked and tree share it, and `Widths::stacked_dragged` takes the switch so the stack line stops at the section. The drag stores what the layout made of the pointer (`App::agents_dragged` lays out again with the wanted rows and keeps the height it got), so every layout clamps it the same way. Rows (`ui::AgentEntry`, `ui::agent_rows`: two lines each, `pitch.max(2)`, no sticky button, scrolled by item like the other lists) are in sidebar order (`App::agent_places`: projects from `project::sidebar_order`, then workspaces, tabs and panes), not most urgent first: rows that move are hard to click, and the icon already says which one waits. One row per pane, so a click focuses that pane in a split tab. The first line is the status icon and `project › workspace` (the workspace only when the project has more than one, `› default` says nothing); the second is the agent's kind, then the model and the context percentage as the tab line shows them (the same `model` and `context` switches; `ui::workspaces::draw_details` takes the kind as a lead that is never cut). Memory is left out: it is only measured for panes on screen (see Memory), and widening that gate would run a thread every 2 s per agent off screen. A narrow column cuts the model first, then drops it, then drops the workspace (kept while 4 cells of it fit), then cuts the project; the icon stays. The pane in focus gets the surface and the `▌`. A press grabs the row (`Grab::Agent`), a release on it jumps (`App::jump_to_pane`: `goto`, which unfolds in the tree and closes the compact menu, then `Tab::focus`); there is no drag to reorder, since the order is computed. A right-click opens a menu with **mark as pending review** or **mark as reviewed** for that pane (see Agent status). No agents shows `no agents running` rather than hiding the section, so the lists do not jump when the first agent starts. Every agent with an `agents::activity::Pane` status is listed, including launch-only and custom agents that emit OSC 7501.
- **Resize pointer** (`host/pointer.rs`, #168): a border that drags lit up cyan under the mouse, but the pointer stayed as it was, so nothing said it could be dragged until one tried. The outer terminal is asked for a pointer shape with OSC 22 (`ESC ] 22 ; <name> ESC \`, kitty's spec, with CSS names): `ew-resize` on the column borders (projects, workspaces, the right panel's) and on dividers between panes side by side, `ns-resize` on the stack line, the agents section's line and dividers between panes one above the other. `App::pointer` works the shape out from the state on demand, like the cyan: a border or divider being dragged (`Drag::Border`, `Drag::Divider` in `App::drag`) keeps its shape wherever the pointer is; otherwise the border or divider under `hover`, unless an overlay is open, another drag or a selection is going on, the pointer is in the side panel, or the compact menu covers the pane; anything else is the default. The server asks for it in `Server::flush`, every turn, per window, and sends it only when it differs from what that window was last told (`Client::shape`, unknown at attach, so a window starts with the default: a remote window that lost its link mid-drag gets its pointer back on reconnecting). Pushing it as a host write from the code paths that change hover or drags was rejected: every way the state changes (a release, `Esc`, an overlay opening, a panic resetting the interaction) would have to remember it, and host writes go to every window alike. `Esc` now ends a border or divider drag where it is (it used to reach the pane), as it cancels row drags, and before the `Esc` that closes a side panel, so dragging the panel's own border and pressing `Esc` keeps the panel open; going back to the width before the drag was left out, since the panes already followed it live. The prefix key ends border, divider, row and pane drags too as it opens the keys menu: these drags would otherwise go on under the menu, keeping the resize arrow over it (the mouse goes on reaching a drag while an overlay is open). Text and file-line selections continue.
  - **Which terminals** (checked in the sources and docs, October 2026; not live): Ghostty (from 1.0; CSS names, an unknown or empty name is ignored with a warning, and its default shape with mouse reporting on is `text`), kitty (from 0.31; CSS names, an empty name resets to its configured default) and foot (from 1.12; CSS and X cursor names, an empty or unknown name resets). Those three get it; everything else gets nothing (`host::pointer::detect` answers `None`). iTerm2 (3.5) and xterm (367) honour OSC 22 but with X11 cursor names of their own, which would mean a name per terminal and checking how each resets; WezTerm, Konsole, Alacritty and VTE do not implement it. Unknown OSCs are dropped by the terminals we know, but a pointer left as a resize arrow over the panes is worse than none, so the rule is the one `host::notify::detect` follows: only where it is known to work. The reset therefore depends on the terminal (`Terminal::reset`): Ghostty gets `text`, since an empty name leaves its pointer stuck and `default` would show an arrow where it showed an I-beam; kitty and foot get the empty name, which brings back their own configured default.
  - **Deciding**: from the XTVERSION reply only (`ghostty …`, `kitty(…)`, `foot(…)`, each at least the version above), the same reply the images and notifications read; all three answer it, also over ssh. The environment is not used: `TERM_PROGRAM=ghostty` is inherited by terminals started from Ghostty. The client decides (it also needs the answer to reset on exit) and sends it in `Hello` (`pointer`, `protocol::VERSION` 3).
  - **Multiplexers**: never inside tmux, screen or Zellij (`detect::in_multiplexer`, or XTVERSION naming tmux). tmux drops OSC 22, so it would need passthrough, which is off by default and, under `on`, dropped while the pane is not visible, so a reset could be lost and the arrow stay; the outer terminal's pointer also belongs to every tmux pane, so an arrow left by cornercase would show over the user's other panes. A pointer is a hint, not worth that risk.
  - **Leaving**: the client writes the reset with the kitty image deletes on every way out (quit, detach, restart, kill-server, a signal, a remote window giving up, a panic on the main thread), since the server may be gone and cannot send it. A window killed outright (`SIGKILL`) can leave the arrow, as it leaves its images; the next window resets it when it attaches.
- **Row menus** (` ⋯ `, `ui::row_menu_button`): every group, project, workspace and tab row has a ` ⋯ ` just left of its `×` (never right of it), shown and lit like the `×` (on hover in wide mode, cyan instead of red; always, dimmed, in compact mode), that opens on press the menu a right-click opens there, anchored at the pointer (`SidebarHit::Menu`, `GroupMenu`, `WorkspaceHit::WorkspaceMenu`, `TabMenu`, `TreeHit::Menu`, all handled by the same `open_*_menu` as the right-click). The menus were only behind the right button, which nothing on screen hinted at. Three dots rather than a gear: a gear says settings, three dots say more actions for this item, the convention of the sidebars it resembles (Notion, Linear, GitHub), and `⚙` is an emoji some fonts draw two cells wide. Its column (2 cells, 4 in compact mode) is reserved in `Band::room` like the `×`'s, so hovering never reflows a row and never hides its status icon or `↓n`; names lose 2 cells. Rows narrower than `MIN_MENU_ROW_WIDTH` (20) get no ` ⋯ ` and keep those cells, since a 16-column list needs them for the names and the right-click still opens the menu. A workspace being removed shows neither.
- **Counts** (`counts` in `config.json`, Settings → UI, on by default): a project row ends with its number of workspaces, `(n)`, and a collapsed group with its number of projects; off, neither shows (`View::counts`), for those who find the numbers noise. One switch for both, since they read the same.
- **Tabs above the pane** (`tabs` in `config.json`, Settings → UI: `sidebar`, the default, or `top`; `config::Tabs`; #119): with `top`, the active workspace's tabs leave the sidebar for a bar of `tab_bar::HEIGHT` (2) rows on top of the pane (`Areas::tab_bar`, cut off `Areas::pane` by `Areas::with_tab_bar`, which `App::layout` and `ui::draw` both apply, so the PTYs are sized without it). Tabs in the sidebar only mattered for the workspace on screen, yet took a row each (two with the details line) plus `+ Tab`, pushing projects and workspaces off the list. `sidebar` is the default so nobody's layout changes on update. The first row is the tabs (`tab_bar::Strip`: status icon, name cut at 24, a split tab's muted `+n` as on its row (`TabEntry::others`), then a ` ⋯ ` and a ` × ` whose cells are always reserved and that show on hover, lit like the rows' (`Strip::menu`, `Strip::close`; `ui::row_menu_button` is not reused, since it leaves out rows under `MIN_MENU_ROW_WIDTH` and a tab is usually narrower), ` + ` after them), the active one on the surface and bold; the second is the details of the focused pane (`App::tab_bar_view`, not the tab's: in a split it follows the pane you are in; a shell pane shows only its memory, and nothing with the memory off), in the muted grey, with the same `model`/`context`/`memory` switches. A path there for shells was rejected: the prompt already shows the folder. Both rows are always there, even with one tab or a shell, so switching tabs never resizes the PTYs. Too many tabs reserve ` ‹ ` / ` › ` at the ends (` + ` goes to the right edge); they and the wheel scroll by one tab, `follow` brings the active tab into view when the focus changes (and starts over at the first tab when the workspace changes), and a drag held on an arrow scrolls like `↑ n more`. A press grabs a tab as a `Target::Tab` row (`RowDrag`), so click, drag and `Esc` work as in the lists; the landing (`Strip::drop`) is a cyan `│` between tabs (`ui::Drag::Bar`), and the drop goes through `move_before` like `Spot::Tab`. The sidebar learns the tabs are elsewhere from `Areas::tab_bar` (`App::drawn`, as with the tree): `ui::TabLines::new(lines, listed)` leaves out tab rows and `+ Tab` from the workspaces list, and `TreeShape::tab_bar` ends the tree at workspaces, which lose their fold arrow (nothing to fold) and show `agents::activity::attention` like folded ones. Compact mode ignores the setting: its bar already names the tab and its menu keeps listing them, and two bars would cost rows a phone does not have. No project, no bar (the rows stay, so the pane does not jump when the first project opens).
- **Icon bar** (`icon_bar` in `config.json`, Settings → UI, off by default; `ui/activity.rs`, `ui/icons.rs`): from 90 columns up, the buttons under the lists (` Issues `, ` Changes `, ` Files `, ` TODO `, ` Settings `, ` Usage `, ` Quit `, and ` Remote ` and the update when they show) become a column of `activity::WIDTH` (4) cells on the left edge, like an editor's activity bar, and the footers go, so the lists reach the bottom. The footer cost every layout four to six rows and spelled out seven buttons in words. `full_layout` carves the bar off before the columns (`Areas::activity`); `projects_column` and `workspaces_column` give the footer rows no height, `one_column` and `stack_room` no footer, so every other rect keeps its meaning and an empty `Areas::settings` (or `issues`, `todo_button`…) simply hits nothing. Border drags subtract the bar's width (`App::drag_border`). Off by default, like `tabs`, so nobody's layout changes on update, and because the icons need a font the program cannot check for.
  - **Icons** are Nerd Fonts' codicons, VS Code's own: Ghostty, kitty and WezTerm include them, other terminals need a Nerd Font set as their font, else they show as boxes. Whether a font has a glyph cannot be asked (a missing one still takes a cell), so the switch is the user's. With it on, the compact bar's `≡ ± ▤ ☐ ⌕`, the search bar's `⌕`, the changes filter's `⌕` and the files panel's `▤` take the same set (`icons::Glyphs`, `PLAIN` or `NERD`, carried by `View`, `changes::View` and `files::View`); off, nothing changes. Each icon sits in a 3-cell slot (`▌`, icon, blank): a blank after it lets Ghostty and kitty draw it over two cells, the largest a character gets. Ghostty sizes icons to its icon height (1.2 times the height of capital letters, its `adjust-icon-height`), which no program can change: a terminal has no font size per cell. Larger icons would need pictures through the kitty graphics protocol, which tmux, a cornercase pane and most terminals would not show; left out.
  - **Places** (`activity::items`, which drawing and `activity::hit` share): Issues, Changes, Files, TODO and Flow (codicon `type-hierarchy`, while the active workspace has a run) from the top, one row apart; Quit, Settings, Usage, then Remote and the update from the bottom. A button that does not apply (Changes outside git, Issues and Files without a project) is left out and the others close up. A short bar drops the top buttons first, then the bottom ones from the update down, so Quit and Settings are the last to go; a bar under five rows has room for Quit alone.
  - **Indicators** keep the footer's: the number of changed files (a filled circle with the number, Material Design's `numeric-N-circle`, `9+` from ten), TODO's grey dot (`todo_dot`) and Remote's green one, at the icon's bottom-right corner, the cell under its second half. The bar's border follows them, so Ghostty draws them in one cell, smaller than the icon; under the icon, followed by a blank, they were as large as the icons. The exact count is in the tooltip.
  - **Names on hover**: the button under the pointer is named beside the bar, on the surface (`activity::draw_tooltip`, drawn after everything but overlays), not while an overlay is open or a drag goes on (`sidebar_hovered`), since icons alone are guessed. An open panel or dialog gets the selection's cyan `▌` and a cyan icon; hover fills the slot like a row, Quit turns red, the update is always cyan.
  - **Colours** (#275): an icon at rest is `View::soft` (see Soft text), a step brighter than the muted grey so the bar does not read as disabled; hovered, and in its tooltip, it is the terminal's own foreground (`Color::Reset`), bold, which is readable on its background by definition. They were `Color::Gray` and `Color::White` (ANSI 7 and 15), which nothing checked: on light themes they were light greys or white on white, and Solarized Light's ANSI 7 is its own highlight background (1.1:1). Icons are thin strokes, so they need more than letters: soft text asks 3:1, WCAG's minimum for non-text components, where grey text asks 2:1. The cyan of an open panel, Quit's red, the update's cyan and the counts' cyan and green stay the theme's own accents: light themes draw them for their background (measured: GitHub Light's cyan 4.9:1, One Light's 4.0:1, Tokyo Night Day's 4.3:1, Catppuccin Latte's teal 3.3:1, Solarized Light's cyan and green 2.9:1), and an open panel also has its `▌` and bold, so its state never rests on colour alone. Swapping them for fixed palette colours was left out: cyan is the selection colour of the whole UI, and changing it in the bar alone would make the bar disagree with the rows.
  - The simulation lists the row and says it needs a real cornercase: the site's renderer has no Nerd Font.
- **Side panels**: the changes, files, TODO and Flow panels share the column right of the pane (`Areas::changes`, `Widths::changes`, `Border::Changes`), one at a time (`App::side`). An open file can take the whole window instead (see Files panel). Flow (#263) shows only while the active workspace has a run (`App::panel_shown`), so a workspace without one gets the pane's width back and returning shows it again. It has no footer button or compact bar slot, since it exists only while a run does: it opens from the run workspace's menu (**show flow**), `w f`, the icon bar's Flow slot, and when a run starts from the window. Its flag is saved beside `todo` and `files` and applied after the runs are restored, so it opens only when the active workspace still has one.
- **Folding** (`Project::collapsed`, `Workspace::collapsed`, saved in the session; the tree draws both, the workspaces list draws the workspaces', #294): new projects and workspaces start unfolded. `follow` unfolds the active project and workspace, and in the tree its group, when the focus changes; a click on a row and `goto` (search) do too, for when the focus stays the same. Other layouts leave a collapsed group alone, as before. The first follow after a restore (`followed` still default) unfolds nothing, so saved folds survive. The workspaces list (side by side, stacked, compact) draws `Workspace::collapsed` with the tree's arrow, `▾`/`▸`, in the row's lead (`[▌, arrow]`, 4 cells; the name moved from 2 to 4 cells, level with the tab icons, as in the tree), always visible rather than on hover, since a click that folds should be findable and touch screens have no hover. The whole lead, the row's full height, is the target (`ui::workspaces::fold_button`, shared by drawing and hit testing; `WorkspaceHit::Fold` grabs the row with `fold: true`, so a drag from the arrow still reorders). One flag serves both views, so a fold made in the tree shows in the list. A folded workspace lists no tabs and no `+ Tab` (`TabLines::folded`, read by drawing, hit testing, drops and `follow` through `workspace_rows`); its shells keep running. With `tabs` on top there is nothing to fold: the lead keeps a blank 2-cell slot and folds are ignored there. A folded active workspace takes the surface and the `▌`, since its active tab row is hidden (the tree's nearest shown ancestor rule, only for folded rows); no `│` hangs under it. The memory gate skips a collapsed workspace's tabs in the lists too.
- Lists scroll by item with the wheel, show ` ↑ n more` / ` ↓ n more`, and keep the `+` button sticky at the bottom. The scroll follows the active item only when it changes (`App::follow`), otherwise the wheel could never move away from it.
- The active row (project, tab, a collapsed group holding the active project, the tree's marked row) gets a surface background (palette 236, or 254 on light themes) and a cyan `▌` in the column's first cell over the row's full height (a tab's second line, a compact band), so every list shares one selection rail on the left edge. `draw_rail` and the tree lines only draw on blank cells, so text pushed left in a narrow column wins over them. Colours use palette indices so they work without truecolor; the brand purple is index 99.
- **Lines** (column borders, the stack and footer separators, split dividers, the changes panel's rules, tree lines, the usage track) take `View::line()`: palette 239, or 250 on light themes, a step quieter than the muted grey, so structure recedes behind text. Hover and drag still light borders and dividers cyan.
- **Tree lines**: tabs hang from their workspace's arrow, in the workspaces list and in the tree: `├` before each tab, `└` before `+ Tab`, `│` on a tab's other rows (its second line, a compact band), below a compact workspace's name and on a landing line among them (`draw_guide`, `among_tabs`, `tree_among_tabs`). In the tree they sit in the workspace's arrow column; projects and workspaces keep only their arrows, since lines at every level made the tree busy.
- **Empty panes**: a workspace without tabs centers `no tab open` and `+ Tab opens a shell here`. With no project at all the pane shows the logo (the favicon's corner and cursor in half blocks, purple 99 and cyan), the wordmark, the tagline and `+ New opens a folder`, or the words alone when the pane is too short (`draw_welcome`); it needs an empty project list, not just no active project.
- **Capitals**: the names of the main UI start with a capital letter, so they read as names rather than as words in a sentence: the column titles (`Projects`, `Workspaces`, `Agents`), the sidebar and footer buttons (`+ New`, `+ Tab`, ` Issues `, ` Changes n `, ` Files `, ` TODO `, ` Settings `, ` Usage `, ` Quit `, `‹ Projects`, `Agents ›`), the panels' titles and tabs (` Files `, `TODO`, `+ New TODO`, `Uncommitted` / `Commits` / `All`, like the settings and issues tabs already were) and every dialog title (`New workspace`, `Remove workspace`, `Keys › Git`…). TODO is in capitals, as the word is written in code. Menu entries, the buttons inside dialogs, placeholders, toasts and messages stay lower case: they are actions and sentences, and a menu of capitalised verbs reads louder than its rows. `Overlay::name` and log targets keep their lower-case ids; a rename has its own title (`Target::rename_title`), since `rename_label` is also its menu entry.
- **Expand and collapse**: what the user reads says *expand* and *collapse* for showing and hiding what a row holds: the changes panel's ` expand all ` / ` collapse all ` (it said ` unfold all ` / ` fold all `), the settings note on a collapsed group, the docs on the tree and the changes panel. They are the words review tools use for the same control (GitLab's **Expand all** / **Collapse all** files, GitHub's collapse on a pull request's diffs, JetBrains' trees), and the ones `cornercase status` already printed for a group; *fold* is an editor's word for regions of code (Vim, VS Code's Fold All), and the panel lists files, not regions. The code keeps `fold`, `folded` and `collapsed` as it had them: renaming the model would churn the code and its tests for no change anyone sees.
- **Grey text** (titles, the footer, the search field, arrows, notes) is `View::muted`, never `Color::DarkGray` directly: `ui::muted` keeps the theme's bright black (ANSI 8) when `HostTheme::muted_is_readable`, else palette 243 (245 on light themes). Readable means its WCAG contrast with the background is at least 2:1, or the background is unknown (nothing to compare with, so as before). Some themes make bright black the background or close to it: Solarized Dark (1:1), Warp's adeberry (#2b2b2a on #1d2022, about 1.15:1, the footer and arrows vanished), Nord (1.7:1); One Dark (2.5:1), Dracula (3:1) and Gruvbox (4:1) keep theirs. A known background with an unknown bright black also gets the fixed grey: Warp answers OSC 11 but not OSC 4, and the 256-colour greys are not themed (the surfaces rely on that too). The changes panel's `View` carries the same colour.
- **Soft text** (a row that is not selected, a tab not active, the rest of a breadcrumb, a grey pill, an icon of the icon bar) is `View::soft`, never `Color::Gray` directly: `ui::soft` keeps the theme's white (ANSI 7) when `HostTheme::soft_is_readable`, its contrast being at least 3:1 both with the background (or the background unknown) and with the surface it is also drawn on (palette 236, or 254 on light themes: a selected row, a pill), else palette 250 (240 on light themes, darker there). The surface counts because it is darker than a light background and lighter than a dark one, so a grey that just passes against white (#949494, 3:1) is 2.3:1 on 254. Text that stands out (the selected row, an active tab, a file's name, a header) is the terminal's own foreground (`Color::Reset`), never `Color::White` (ANSI 15), which light themes make white or near it. ANSI 7 is often a light grey on light themes (Solarized Light's is its highlight background, 1.1:1), so rows and pills faded there like the icon bar did (#275). Text that stands out stays `Color::Reset` on a surface too, rather than black or white by `View::light`: some light themes make ANSI 0 a light grey (Tokyo Night Day's `#b4b5b9` is 1.6:1 on 254, where its foreground is 4.6:1). The exceptions are the combo and the queue form, which already picked black or white by `View::light`, and greys used as a background (a hovered grey pill).
- A hovered project, workspace, tab or group row is filled edge to edge with palette 235 (255 on light themes), one step between the background and the surface (`View::row_background`). The active row keeps its surface. No hover fill while an overlay is open or a border or divider is being dragged. The site's palette tints 235 like 236 (`site/src/lib/term/palette.ts`), so it stays between them, and 239, the line colour, so it stays between the surface and the muted grey.
- **Compact mode** below 90 columns (`ui::layout::compact_layout`): a top bar (` ≡ `, `project › workspace › tab`, then ` ± `, ` ▤ `, ` ☐ `, ` ⌕ ` right-aligned; ` ± ` only in git workspaces and ` ▤ ` only with a project, so their slots are left of ` ☐ ` and an empty one merges into the breadcrumb; ` ☐ ` gets a muted `●` to its right, in its own slot, while TODO items are pending, see TODO panel) and a full-screen menu showing one column at a time. Every clickable item is a 3-row band (`Areas::pitch`). Both columns' areas share one rect and `Areas::shown(nav)` blanks the hidden one, so drawing and hit testing reuse the wide-mode code. With the agents section on, it is a third menu (`Nav::Agents`): ` Agents › ` at the right of the projects menu's title opens it, ` ‹ Projects ` (the same `Areas::back`) goes back, and a click on a row closes the menu on that pane.

## Dialogs (`ui/dialog.rs`, `ui/dialog/`, `ui/combo.rs`, `list.rs`, `app/overlay.rs`, `app/dialogs.rs`)

- Dialogs, menus and the toast have rounded borders (`BorderType::Rounded`); a title sits after one border cell (`╭─ settings ─`), bold in the default colour, so it reads as the dialog's heading rather than part of the border.
- A dialog (form, confirmation, picker, settings, issues, usage, update, group style: `Overlay::is_modal`) dims everything behind it (`Modifier::DIM` on the whole buffer before it is drawn), so it reads as on top. A menu or the search does not: they belong to what they were opened from. DIM leaves backgrounds alone, so surfaces stay.
- Lists inside overlays (menus, picker, issues, search results, a settings pick, settings rows) mark the selected row like the sidebar: surface background, a cyan `▌` in its first cell and bold text; a hovered row takes the hover fill. The filled cyan bar they had was loud, and two of them lit at once (the selected row and the hovered one). The active tab of a tab row (settings, issues, the changes panel: `tab_style`) is cyan bold on the surface.
- **A left press outside a dialog closes it** (`App::outside_dialog`, before each dialog's own mouse handling), like its cancel button (`cancel_form`), so a busy one (`Overlay::busy`: creating a worktree, installing an update, checking a token, starting an issue) stays open. Menus and the search already closed that way; dialogs only closed with their buttons or `Esc`, which is not what anyone expects of a modal. Where the dialog is comes from `ui::Overlay::area`, which uses the geometry functions the drawing uses (`form_area`, `picker_area`, `usage_area`, `issues_area`, `settings_area`), and a test per dialog checks its border is drawn at that area's corners. It closes the whole dialog, not one level as `Esc` does in the issues browser or a settings pick. What was typed in a form is dropped, as with `Esc`; keeping forms with text open was considered and left out: one rule for every dialog is easier to learn than a click that sometimes does nothing.
- Dialog buttons are pills on the surface (`dialog_button_style`): the submit one cyan bold, the others grey; hover fills them cyan or grey. Without a background they read as coloured words, not as something to click.
- **Comboboxes** (`list::Combo`, `ui/combo.rs`): a choice among a few named options is a field showing the chosen one with a `▾`, whose list opens below it like a web page's select (above it when it does not fit), at most `COMBO_ROWS` (8) rows with ` ↑ n more ` / ` ↓ n more ` on its border and the wheel to scroll. A click, `Space`, `↑` or `↓` opens it; `↑` `↓` `PageUp` `PageDown` `Home` `End` move, typing filters (case-insensitive, a substring, as the search does), `Enter` or a click chooses, `Esc` or a click elsewhere closes only the list. The model (options, choice, and while open a `list::Search` for the filter, selection and scroll) is a shared leaf so other dialogs can take it (settings' picks, the issues browser's agent and place pickers, the changes panel's branch picker); the field, the list and their hit testing share the geometry in `ui/combo.rs`. The open list belongs to its dialog: `ui::Overlay::area` includes it, so a click on a list that reaches past the dialog's border chooses instead of closing the dialog. A list over the form rather than in place (as the issues browser's pickers are) was left out: the form's other rows stay in view.

## Hierarchy (`project.rs`, `project/group.rs`, `project/hierarchy.rs`, `app/hierarchy.rs`, `app/tabs.rs`, `app/menus.rs`, `app/sidebar/drag.rs`)

- Optional group > project (a canonicalized folder) > workspace (the project folder, or its own git worktree) > tab > panes. Every level has an id from one counter; menus, forms and background jobs refer to ids, never indices.
- **Lookups and project order belong to the model** (`project/hierarchy.rs`, re-exported from `project`): `Hierarchy` on `[Project]` finds projects, workspaces, tabs and panes by id and walks all children. Each level also has `index_of`, and a tab has `pane_by`, `pane_by_mut` and `layout_by_index`; `Project::label` owns the project label. A trait gives method syntax without wrapping the project's vector or changing indexed access: Rust allows no inherent impl on a slice. Calls on `self.projects` borrow that field alone, so callers can still write launches, the toast and host output. Trait methods avoid names of slice and vector methods, whose inherent methods would silently win. The module stays private because `app` already has a child named `hierarchy`. Lookups return positions for callers that then change `App`; those positions are used within the event, never stored in menus, dialogs or jobs. Lookups never filter closing projects or removing workspaces; each caller keeps its own eligibility checks. `workspace_in` and `tab_in` also check the named parent. `tab_index`, `tab_in` and `tab_of_pane` share one walk.
- **Plain references end lookup borrows when their last use ends**: an `if let` or `match` over an iterator's `find` can keep its opaque iterator temporary alive until the block ends, borrowing `projects` even after the pane's last use. That made copying a free function taking host writes and the toast separately. `pane_by_mut` returns an `Option<&mut Term>`, so the output handler can call `App::copy` once the pane is no longer used. New code uses these lookups instead of finding ids inline, including through the flat iterators. `split_pane` still looks its tab up twice: shared `tab_with_pane` reads the pane's folder before `spawn`, then `tab_with_pane_mut` inserts it afterwards. `spawn` borrows all of `App` for ids and sizing, so neither a reference into `projects` nor an unchecked position is carried across it. A spawner borrowing separate fields is a later change. `watch_agents` keeps nested loops because it needs visibility and ids from every level beside config and stats.
- Labels: custom name, else folder name (project), branch or `default` (workspace), foreground program (tab).
- **A split tab is named after its first pane** (`Tab::label`: the first leaf of `Tab::layout`, the top-left pane), not the active one, so the name stays put while the focus moves between panes; it followed the focus before (#40), and the row changed name on every click. Its row ends with a muted `+n`, the number of other panes (`TabEntry::others`), also for a custom name, fitted first like `↓n` (`Tags::fit`) so a long name is cut before it. Only the rows show it; search and the compact bar's breadcrumb keep the plain label. Model and context still follow the active pane (`Tab::context`), since they describe the agent you are looking at.
- **A tab's program** (`term::program`): a known agent (`agents::detect`) gives its kind; for an interpreter (`node`, `bun`, `deno`, `python`, `python3`, `ruby`) the whole argv is matched, for anything else only `argv[0]`, so `less claude` stays `less`. An interpreter whose first non-flag argument is a script (contains `/` or has a script extension, no whitespace, and no `-e`, `--eval`, `-p`, `--print`, `-c` or `-m` before it, so modules and inline code keep the interpreter's name) gives the script's stem: npm installs run `node /…/bin/codex`. Linux `comm` is the main thread's name, which recent Node renames to `node-MainThread`; that becomes `node`. Its PTY test runs a copy of `/bin/bash` named `node` (`comm` comes from the executed file; a symlink to dash would do on Linux, but macOS names the process after the target).
- Closing kills processes; removal waits for the shell to exit, never synchronous. A pane goes when its reader hits the PTY's end (`Exited`) or, on any `App::refresh`, when `Term::exited` finds the shell reaped (`try_wait`), whichever comes first. Both, because the PTY's end only comes once every process has closed the terminal: one that left the shell's session and ignores SIGHUP (`nvim --embed` outliving its UI, `nohup`, `setsid`) would keep the tab as `?` forever. Like tmux, the pane goes with the shell; the reader thread and the master's fd stay until that process exits. `Term::kill` skips a reaped shell, since portable-pty signals the pid unconditionally and it may have been reused. Closing a project asks first (`Overlay::CloseProject`), since it stops every shell in it, agents included; a tab or a plain workspace closes at once in wide mode and asks first in compact mode. Closing the last tab keeps the workspace; closing the last workspace keeps the project.
- **Groups**: one level, no nesting; a new one goes last. A project holds `group: Option<u64>`; the projects column is `ui::sidebar_rows` (loose projects first, then per group a gap, the header and, unless collapsed, its projects). `project::group_indices` maps missing groups to loose projects; `project::sidebar_order` gives the project order with every group open for the agents section, the Every project changes scope and project stepping. Tests keep that order equal to the project rows of the unfolded UI. Drawing, hit testing and `App::follow` share the UI row list. A collapsed group holding the active project gets the `▌` on its header. Deleting a group (the `×` on its header, or its menu) asks first (`Overlay::DeleteGroup`). A group holding projects asks what to do with them: **keep projects** (the submit, so `Enter` keeps the old behaviour and stops nothing) only ungroups them; **close projects** (`ui::Confirm::extra`, a grey button left of the submit, `FormHit::Extra`) closes each of them like `close_project`, then deletes the group once they are gone (`App::deleting_groups`: a closing project stays in its group until its shells exit, so it never jumps to the loose ones for a frame, nor shows as moved in the log; `View` and `sidebar_rows` take a `group` pointing at a deleted group as loose, so leaving the id dangling would not have helped; a project added or moved into the group meanwhile that is not closing makes it stop waiting as it enters (`App::cancel_pending_group_deletion`, on every way in: a drag, **move to group**, the picker), even when it closes before the others, so it is never deleted unasked), since emptying a group meant closing its projects one by one. The message says how many tabs that stops; folders and worktrees stay on disk. An empty group keeps the plain **delete**. The labels come from `App::dialog_labels`, live from the group's projects (closing ones left out), so drawing and hit testing agree. Icons are a fixed set of one-cell symbols (`project::group::GROUP_ICONS`, no emoji or Nerd Font glyphs, whose width varies); colours are palette indices (`project::group::GROUP_COLOURS`, no greys, black or white, which vanish on some themes). A new group takes the first of eight presets (`project::group::GROUP_STYLES`, chosen by `project::group::preset`) that no group has, the same icon and colour, else they repeat in order, and nothing opens: the icon and colour modal used to open after the name, a question asked at the moment one only wanted a group, and it is one click away in the group's menu (` ⋯ ` or right-click → **icon and colour**), whose clicks apply at once. The presets start with blue, the favourite colour in every one of the ten countries of YouGov's 2015 survey, then green, purple and red (the runners-up there), orange, yellow, pink and teal; each has its own shape, so groups differ without colour. Each is in `GROUP_ICONS` and `GROUP_COLOURS`, so the modal marks it.
- **Adding to a group**: the group's menu starts with **add project**, which opens the picker (`Overlay::Picker { group: Some(_) }`, titled ` add project `) in the folder above the group's first project; what it opens lands in the group (an already open project moves there) and the group expands. `+ new project` never guesses a group from the active project: a new project landing in a group you did not pick would surprise.
- **VS Code workspaces** (`project/vscode.rs`): the picker lists `*.code-workspace` files after the folders, tagged `vs code`, also while filtering (folders first, then the match rank), so a quick `Enter` on a filter goes into a folder rather than importing; choosing one imports it once, never linked (cornercase does not write the file nor watch it). The group is the file's stem, reused when a group has that name (re-importing brings in folders added since), else a new one with the next preset. Each folder opens as a project in it, in order, the first one active; a folder already open moves into the group; its `name` (only when it differs from the folder's name) becomes the project's custom name when the project has none: a new one, or an open one never renamed; a name given in cornercase is never overwritten. `path` is relative to the file's folder, `uri` is read only as a local `file://` path, `file:///…` or `file://localhost/…` (another host or scheme is skipped, never read as a relative path); missing folders are skipped and counted in the toast, and a new group left empty is removed. A folder whose shell cannot start (`open_into` fails) does not end the import, as in the restore: it is logged with its cause, counted apart from the missing ones (`n could not open, see server.log`, with the bug icon) and the next folder goes on. The toast counts distinct projects (a folder listed twice, `api` and `./api`, opens once and counts once) and says `nothing imported from <name>` when none opened, since the empty new group is gone by then. A UTF-8 byte order mark is skipped, and every read error, not only a JSON one, names the file. VS Code accepts comments and trailing commas, `serde_json` does not, so `strip_jsonc` removes both outside strings first, rather than adding a JSON5 crate. A file that cannot be read keeps the picker open with the error.
- Menus are one `Overlay::Menu { actions: Vec<MenuAction> }`, labels from `App::menu_label`; a submenu (move to group) replaces the menu at the same spot. `+ New` opens a menu (open project / new group); it was `+ new project`, which named only one of the two. The workspaces column's `+ New` (in every layout, and a project's `+ New` row in the tree) opens one too, for that project: **new workspace**, what `+ New workspace` did, and **start a flow…** (#263). A flow makes a workspace, so offered in the projects' `+ New` it read as if it made a project.
- **Reordering** (drag): project, group, workspace and tab rows act on release. A press grabs the row (`Drag::Row` in `App::drag`, holding a `Target`); leaving the row's rect, or releasing outside it, makes it a drag, otherwise the release is the click (activate, collapse), so a cancelled drag changes nothing and the compact menu stays open while dragging. `×` and `+` still act on press. Releasing outside the list, `Esc` or another button cancels; no hover while dragging.
- Where it lands (`ui::sidebar_drop`, `ui::workspace_drop`) comes from the pointer and the layout without the line, so the line never feeds back: the row under the pointer gives its slot (before it when above the dragged row, after when below); a group with its projects and a workspace with its tabs are one block; a group header takes a project in first (last when collapsed); a gap is the end of the section above; tabs stay in their workspace, workspaces in their project (moving a shell or a worktree elsewhere was left out). The line goes on a neighbouring gap, on the line above or below the visible rows at an edge, else as an inserted one-line `Landing` row; it is indented where a grouped project or a tab would start.
- Holding a drag on `↑ n more` / `↓ n more` scrolls one item every `AUTO_SCROLL_EVERY` (150 ms); `App::tick` shortens the server's wait meanwhile. Moving only changes `Vec` positions (`project::move_before` keeps the active index on its item), so ids, menus and jobs are unaffected and the session saves the order as is; worktrees found outside cornercase go last.

## Splits (`project/split.rs`, `app/panes.rs`)

- A binary tree (`Leaf` / `Split { dir, ratio, first, second }`). Right-click in a pane opens split/close/right-click-passthrough; this works even when the program captured the mouse, since almost no program uses the right button.
- A vertical divider is followed by a blank column so text does not touch it. Inactive panes are dimmed (configurable). A left click on an inactive pane only focuses it. Dividers drag like column borders.
- **Every pane keeps its minimum** (`MIN_COLS`×`MIN_ROWS`, the size a split needs on each side). `Node::min_size` is what a subtree needs along a direction: a leaf its minimum, a split along that direction both sides plus the divider's gap, a split across it the larger side. Each split hands its sides at least their minimum when its area allows it (`project::split::divide`), so a client smaller than the one the splits were made on squeezes the panes down to their minimum, never to nothing; the stored `ratio` does not change, so growing back restores the proportions. A divider drag is clamped to the same minimum of both sides, nested splits included (`Divider` carries them, `ratio_at` clamps), so the stored ratio is what the screen shows. Before (#33), the drag left 1 cell on each side, and that side could be a nested split whose panes then got 0 rows: invisible, unclickable, and the active one still took the keys.
- **A tab too small for every pane shows only its active pane** over the whole tab (`Node::has_room`, `Node::visible`), until there is room again: a phone attaching to splits made on a big screen, a narrow window. Drawing, hit testing, `App::resize` and `control` all go through `visible` (`Tab::shown`, `Tab::rect`, `Tab::pane_at`), so keys and clicks never reach a pane that is not on screen. The hidden panes keep running at their last size (their PTYs are not resized), there are no dividers, and the pane menu offers no split (`Tab::can_split`), since a new pane would be hidden too. Squeezing every pane to 1 cell was rejected: visible but useless. A single pane always shows, whatever the size.
- **Moving panes** (#107): a right-button drag from one pane onto another moves it there, keeping every program running (only the tree changes, then the PTYs are resized). The right button because it is already cornercase's in a pane (the menu), whatever the program does with the mouse; a left drag is the program's or the selection, panes have no title bar to grab (one would cost a row per pane), and modifier drags do not arrive reliably (Cmd never, Ctrl and Alt are taken by some outer terminals). So the pane menu now opens on the release, when the pointer never left the pane (`Drag::Pane` in `App::drag`, like the rows' `RowDrag`). Where it lands is `project::split::Place::at` on the pane under the pointer: the middle third in both directions swaps the two leaves (`Node::moved` keeps the shape and ratios); elsewhere the nearest edge, relative to the pane's size, removes the pane (`Node::remove`) and splits the target on that side at half (`Node::insert`, `before` for left and above). `Tab::landing` offers only moves whose tree still `has_room`; drawing (`TabView::landing`: the half the pane would take, or the whole target, on the surface with ` move here ` / ` swap `) and the drop share it. The moved pane becomes the active one. `Esc`, another button, or a release on the same pane or outside the panes cancels. Menu entries (move pane, flip a split) and Ctrl+drag were considered and left out: one gesture covers every case, flipping included (drop a pane on its neighbour's bottom edge).
- A pane can still have no room when the whole pane area is empty. Its PTY is sized at least 1×1, and mouse events for it are dropped (`pane_cell` returns `None`), never clamped into an empty range: `clamp` panics when `min > max`, which drops the event and shows the bug toast (see Panics).

## Search (`project/search.rs`, `project/search/said.rs`, `list.rs`, `app/search_dialog.rs`, `app/search_dialog/sessions.rs`, `app/control/search.rs`, `cli/search.rs`, #272)

- **Candidates are built outside `App`** (#195): `project::search::candidates(groups, projects, config, home)` takes what it reads, so its tests need no `App`; `App::search_candidates` passes its fields. Ties among projects keep the order of `projects`, not the sidebar order the next bullet describes: they differ after **move to group** or **add project**, and changing the order is a visible change that needs its own issue.
- One global search across groups, projects, workspaces (label and branch) and tabs (label and their workspace's keys). Ranking: exact, prefix, substring; ties by kind (group, project, workspace, tab) then sidebar order. A group result expands it and activates its first project. Results are recomputed on every key and draw; the query is never kept after closing.
- **In sessions** (#272): the search has a second mode, `project::search::Mode::Sessions`, that looks in what was said instead of in names. `Overlay::Search` holds a `project::search::Finder` (the field, the mode and an `Enter` waiting for an answer, as the files search's `Query::enter`). It opens in names, from `/` or a click, and in sessions only from `f s`: the mode is not remembered, so the bar always starts as it did, and a second way to type queries (the issue offered a `>` prefix) was left out. `Tab` and the 3-cell button at the right end of the bar (`ui::mode_button`, the geometry drawing and hits share, lit cyan in sessions like the files panel's `▤`) switch it and clear the selection. An empty bar says what it searches (a muted placeholder). The glyph is `❯` (plain) or the codicon `comment-discussion` (Nerd): `“` and `»` are ambiguous width.
- **What is searched** (`project::search::said`, pure, no `app` or `ui`): per pane, its agent's transcript (`agents::context::Record::said`, newest first, the same six records `read --last-message` reads: Claude Code's human prompts and queued prompts and its text blocks, Codex's `UserMessage` items and replies, Gemini CLI's real prompts and replies, opencode's text parts that are not synthetic or a compaction summary, Grok Build's `chat_history.jsonl` with its `<user_query>` wrapper stripped, Cursor CLI's turns from its chat store: user text and assistant steps, never thinking or tools) and then the pane's whole history, shells included, from the bottom up (`Emulator::text`: the formatter's plain, unwrapped text of the active screen with its scrollback, so a query cut by a soft wrap still matches; the same call `last_lines` makes). Codex's role-user `response_item` is not read: it also carries the injected AGENTS.md and environment context, and repeats each prompt that its `item_completed` event already holds. Tool results, sidechains and meta prompts are left out. Matching is `text::Needle`, a literal compiled case-insensitive (like names, always, and with Unicode folding), one match per message or screen line, `MAX_PER_PANE` (20) per pane and `MAX_MATCHES` (300) in all, in sidebar order (`project::sidebar_order`, then workspaces, tabs and panes), skipping projects closing and workspaces not `open()`. `Found::capped` says a match was really left out (one more was seen), not that the count reached the cap. A screen line that the transcript's matching messages already hold (normalised: whitespace collapsed, leading `●⎿>›-*` marks dropped, lower case) is dropped, since Claude Code and Codex print their replies and prompts on screen too.
- **Off the UI thread, with the corpus on it**: `Emulator` is `!Send` and lives on the server's main thread, so the text of each pane is read there (`App::build_sources`) and only the matching runs in a job (`App::spawn_job`, `search sessions`, up to 8 scoped threads pulling sources from an atomic index as the files text search does, a cancel flag checked between messages and lines). Formatting tens of MB on every key would be the cost, so the corpus (`said::Sessions::corpus`) is built on the first non-empty query and reused for `CORPUS_FOR` (5 s), and each pane's text is cached by `Term::output_at` and its size, so an idle pane is formatted once. A newer query cancels the running one and its answer is dropped by its generation; a job that never answers is given up on after 30 s, as the files text search. The answer is kept for its query only (`Sessions::answer`), the list shows `searching…` meanwhile, and an `Enter` pressed before it comes opens the first match when it does. The query never reaches the log (`job.note` says only how many matched), and the cache is dropped as soon as no sessions search is open (`ask_sessions`, every refresh), since it can hold megabytes per pane.
- **Results and the reveal**: two lines per result (`ui::SESSIONS_PITCH`, geometry and hit testing take the pitch): the snippet (the matching line, control characters as spaces, about 160 characters shifted so the match shows, with `…` at the cut ends) with the match in cyan, then `who · prompt|reply|screen` and `project › workspace › tab` cut at the start. Choosing one goes to the pane (`jump_to_pane`) and reveals the match (`Emulator::reveal`): it formats the text again and takes the occurrence whose line is nearest the one found, since history trimmed at the top shifts lines in between (the newest when the match came from a transcript, so a reply the screen also shows is highlighted, else the pane is only focused), maps the logical line to its rows by walking `is_wrap_continuation` over `Point::Screen` rows, turns character offsets into rows and columns with `unicode-width` (a wide character that does not fit wraps early), selects through Ghostty's own selection, which already draws as the selected cells, checks the selected text is the match (else it clears and answers false) and scrolls the viewport to a third of the pane's height above it. A press in the pane starts a new selection and removes it. Limits: a full-screen program's pane is searched as shown, since the main screen's history is unreachable while the alternate screen is up; and a program that reads the mouse is put back at the bottom by `snapshot`, which loses the reveal.
- **`cornercase search TEXT…`** (`control/search.rs`, `app/control/search.rs`, `cli/search.rs`) answers with the same matches: a `search` request (an older server refuses it by name, as every new command) builds the same sources without the cache, runs the job without a cancel flag and answers when it ends (`Stage::Reading`, so a client that leaves drops it). Text is one tab-separated row per match under a header (`pane who source place line text`, `-` for a missing value, control characters as spaces, new columns only at the end, written through `print_out`); `--json` prints `{"matches": [...], "capped": bool}` with each match's tab, workspace and project ids and, for a transcript message, when it was written. No match prints the header alone and exits 0: the repository keeps exit 1 for errors, and an empty answer is not one (grep's convention was rejected for that). An empty query is an error. The query is logged by its size only.

## Folder picker (`project/picker.rs`, `list.rs`, `app/picker_dialog.rs`)
